# EXHORTATION TO
## THE BENEVOLENT READER
### *By a certain anonymous student of true philosophy.*

Since for the operation of all wonders two things suffice—namely, Fire and Earth—as Hermes <note>original: "Hermes Philosophorum ille sapientissimus"; referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Egyptian sage and purported founder of alchemy</note>, that wisest of philosophers, says: and regarding Earth, being the subject and Matter of the <term>spagyric (the alchemical art of separating and recombining natural substances)</term> art, the passive element (about which philosophers have written here and there in their books enough and more, and without which the other element cannot work), I have deferred speaking in this place. 

But concerning Fire, which is self-moving and the primary Agent, without which nothing can truly be led from <term>potentiality (a latent or hidden possibility)</term> into <term>Actuality (the fully realized or active state)</term>, since there are few (excepting the author of this treatise) who have investigated and clearly explained its Nature, property, and quality required in the spagyric work, I could not fail to invite you, friend Reader, to the reading of this treatise, so that you might come better to a knowledge of the aforementioned Fire and its hidden [power]... <note>The text breaks here at the syllable "oc-" with the catchword "cul-", indicating the word "occultam" (hidden) continues on the next page.</note>

<summary>This preface introduces the reader to the fundamental alchemical principles of Fire and Earth, positioning Fire as the essential active force required to transform matter from a latent state into a finished product. The author notes that while many have written about matter (Earth), few have properly explained the specific nature of the fire used in the "spagyric" art.</summary>

<keywords>Fire, Earth, Spagyric Art, Hermes Trismegistus, Potentiality and Actuality, Alchemical Theory, Alchemy, Natural Philosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page serves as the opening of the preface (Parænesis). It establishes the philosophical foundation of the work, referencing Hermetic principles of the two elements, Fire and Earth, essential for alchemical transformation.</meta>

# EXHORTATION TO
## THE BENEVOLENT READER
->By a certain Anonymous Student of the true Philosophy.<-

Since two things suffice for the performance of all wonders, namely Fire and Earth, as Hermes <note>original: "Hermes Philoſophorum ille ſapientiffimus" — referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the founder of alchemy.</note>, that wisest of Philosophers, says: and regarding Earth, being the subject and the passive Matter <term>Matter (Latin: *materia*): The physical substance or "patient" upon which the alchemist works.</term> of the spagyric <term>Spagyric: A term for alchemy, specifically the art of separating and recombining the essential components of plants or minerals to create medicines.</term> art (about which Philosophers have written here and there in their books enough and more than enough, and without which the other element cannot operate), I have deferred speaking in this place. 

But concerning Fire, which is the self-moving and principal Agent <term>Agent (Latin: *Agens*): The active force or catalyst that drives a chemical or philosophical process.</term>, without which nothing can truly be led from potentiality into Actuality <note>original: "de potentia ad Actum" — This uses Aristotelian terminology to describe the process of taking a latent possibility and making it a physical reality.</note>, since there are few (except for the author of this Treatise) who have investigated and clearly explained its Nature, property, and quality as required in the Spagyric work, I could not neglect to urge you, friendly Reader, toward the reading of this treatise, so that you might better attain knowledge of the aforementioned Fire and its hidden power—

<summary>This preface introduces the reader to the two fundamental elements of alchemy—Fire and Earth—noting that while Earth is well-documented, this specific text focuses on the rare and essential knowledge of Fire as the active force in transformation.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Fire and Earth, Spagyric art, alchemy, potentiality and actuality, philosophical agent, occult power</keywords>

For Light is a highly formal quality <note>In the philosophy of this era, a "formal quality" refers to the "form" or essential nature of a thing that gives it its specific properties.</note> and an Image diffused from the divine mind into all things, but first in God the Father himself, who is the Father of Lights. Second, it is in His Son, an illuminating and exuberant splendor. Third, in the Holy Spirit, it is a burning brilliance surpassing all understanding, as Dionysius <note>Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite, a highly influential mystical theologian who wrote extensively on the "Celestial Hierarchy" of angels.</note> says of the Seraphim. Fourth, this light diffused in the Angels becomes a shining intelligence and an overflowing joy beyond the bounds of all reason, though received in diverse degrees according to the nature of the receiving intelligence. Fifth, it then descends into the heavenly bodies, where it becomes an abundance of life and an effective propagation, as well as a visible splendor. In Fire, however, there is a certain natural vigor implanted by the heavenly bodies. Finally, in Humans, it becomes a clear discourse of reason and a knowledge of divine things, being entirely rational; from there it passes to the <term>phantasy <note>In early modern psychology, the "phantasy" was the mental faculty that sat between the raw data of the senses and the higher reasoning of the intellect; it was responsible for forming internal images.</note></term>, which is above the senses yet only capable of mental images; finally it reaches the senses, especially that of the eyes,

### TO THE READER.

<margin>9</margin> of the heavens <note>Completing the word "coelorum" from the previous page's catchword "accen-", likely referring to the "accension" or lighting of the heavens.</note>, in that realm it becomes a visible clarity, and is extended through alternating transparent bodies, in which color and a gleaming beauty are formed.
<margin>10</margin> In opaque things, however, it is a certain beneficial and generating power; it penetrates all the way to the center, where its rays are gathered into a narrow space, becoming a dark heat, agonizing and burning.

Therefore, Fire is expanded in heaven, giving light; in hell, it is compressed, dark, and agonizing. In the <term>Center <note>original: "Meditullio". This refers to the middle realm, often the Earth or the human heart, which exists between the heights of heaven and the depths of the underworld.</note></term>, however, it shares in both natures; thus all things experience the vigor of Light and Fire according to their own capacity. Hence the *Philosopher* <note>Alchemical authors often used "The Philosopher" to refer to a supreme authority, usually Hermes Trismegistus or Aristotle.</note> says: *Fire* and *Azoth* <term>Azoth <note>Azoth was considered the "Universal Solvent" or "Universal Cure" in alchemy; the word is formed from the first and last letters of the Latin, Greek, and Hebrew alphabets (A-Z, Alpha-Omega, Aleph-Tav), symbolizing the beginning and the end of all things.</note></term>, that is, the *Animated Spirit*, are sufficient for you. For Fire alone is the whole work and the complete art.

In this <term>Artifice <note>original: "artificio". This refers to the "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) of alchemy—the process of transforming base matter into a perfected state.</note></term>, however, Fire is twofold: internal and external, natural and artificial, of which one is ineffective without the other. For the external fire is the actual instrument, taking the place of the natural Sun; it is the <term>dusky heat <note>original: "furvum". This refers to a low, gentle heat, often likened to the warmth of a brooding hen or the "slow fire" of a lamp furnace.</note></term> that gently [warms] the sphere of the vessel and the internal fire of the matter...

<summary>This page describes the dual nature of Fire: as a celestial light in heaven, a punishing heat in hell, and a generative force on Earth. It introduces the alchemical concept that the "Great Work" requires both an internal natural fire and an external artificial fire to succeed.</summary>

### TO THE READER.

...igniting, warming, and making fertile, no differently than the Sun does for the whole world. Since, therefore, fire is the most powerful part and operation of the <term>spagyric art <note>original: "artificij spagirici". Spagyrics refers to alchemical medicine, specifically the process of separating, purifying, and recombining the components of plants or minerals to create more potent remedies.</note></term>, and the whole of it consists in its <term>Governance <note>original: "Regimine". In alchemy, the 'regimen' refers to the precise management and graduation of heat over time, which is essential for the transformation of matter.</note></term>—without the knowledge of which all labor is useless and empty—it is not without merit that the author of this treatise must be extolled with the highest praise. He is the most distinguished man and most experienced in many sciences, Master Heinrich Khunrath, the most celebrated <term>Doctor of both Medicines <note>original: "utriusque Medicinæ Doctor". This title typically signified a doctorate in both physical medicine and spiritual or alchemical medicine, emphasizing a holistic approach to healing.</note></term>.

He has chosen to bring to light this work concerning the secret, external, and visible <term>fire of the Magi <note>original: "Magorum philosophorumque igne secreto". The Magi were regarded by alchemists as ancient masters of natural and divine secrets; here, Khunrath refers to a specific type of laboratory fire that mimics these ancient mysteries.</note></term> and philosophers. He wished to provide an opportunity for the "sons of doctrine" and the <term>seekers of secrets <note>original: "scrutatoribus arcanorum". 'Arcana' are the deep, hidden mysteries of nature that alchemists sought to uncover through their experiments.</note></term> to investigate it, so that they might better and more perfectly reach their desired goal, gather the fruit of their longing, and be able to dissolve natural things through that fire as if with an <term>anatomical knife <note>original: "cultro Anatomico". Just as a physician uses a scalpel to reveal the internal structures of a body, the alchemist uses controlled fire to "dissect" substances into their primary elements.</note></term>.

Therefore, kind reader, if the inward searching of things and of abstruse <meta>catchword: power (virtus)</meta>

# PHILOSOPHICAL
## Explanation of and concerning
### the
## Outer Philosophical Fire.

One should visit, behold, and read through the paintings and various writings of all true—that is, nature-conforming—chemical philosophers regarding the art of <term>Alchemy <note>original: "Alchymia"</note></term>; there it is found everywhere in great abundance <margin>Of the **EXTERNAL FIRE** and the outer fire.</margin> <note>original: "Quod Lapidis Philosophici Regimen, sine calore Ignis externi per Artem administrato omninò perfici non possit"</note> that the governance of the <term>Philosophers' Stone <note>The legendary goal of alchemy, believed to facilitate the transmutation of base metals into gold and the healing of all illnesses.</note></term> cannot be fully perfected at all without the heat of the external fire administered through Art. That is to say, the procedure for preparing the Philosophers' Stone can never be accomplished without the artificially applied warmth of the outer fire.

<margin>AND THE NECESSITY of its manner. And</margin> Therefore, the ancient chemical philosopher <term>Morienus Romanus <note>A legendary 7th-century alchemist, often portrayed as a hermit who taught the secrets of the Stone to the Umayyad prince Khalid ibn Yazid.</note></term> teaches, from the words of another wise man...

<summary>This page introduces the fundamental necessity of the "Outer Philosophical Fire" in the alchemical process, asserting that the Philosophers' Stone cannot be created without the careful application of external heat. It begins to cite historical authorities like Morienus Romanus to support this technical requirement.</summary>

<keywords>Outer Philosophical Fire, Alchemy, Philosophers' Stone, Morienus Romanus, external heat, alchemical governance, chemical philosophy</keywords>

# Of External Things.

<margin>
The aforementioned manner of fire, how it is principally to be understood. <note>original: "IGNIS MODVS præfatus qualis principaliter intelligendus"</note>

...but rather it should be animated universally <note>original: "Catholice"</note> according to its Essence and Nature, and applied to the Work in a physico-artificial manner. That is to say, much more according to its Essence, Nature, and Properties, Universally—meaning according to its general powers, virtues, and effects—to be given to the Work in a blessed and nature-conforming-artful way.

Namely, what kind of fire that might be, I say, concerning which one finds recorded among the Philosophers in one place and all at once not much that is clear and bright, but only a little for guidance; I will at this time and in this place, with the assistance of the Spirit of the Wisdom of God <note>original: "RUACH HOCHMAH-EL"</note>, <term>treat a little</term> <note>original: "etwz tractirē," shorthand for "etwas tractiren," meaning to discuss or handle a subject.</note> of it.

# Philosophical Fire

Among the Persians, Assyrians, Chaldeans, Medes, and Egyptians, virgins and honored, elderly matrons—likewise among the Greeks in the Delphic Temple—were tasked with maintaining and guarding a constantly enduring and inwardly-burning fire upon the altars of various idols they had fashioned for themselves. This was kept in the <term>Adytis</term> <note>From the Greek *adyton*, meaning "not to be entered"; refers to the innermost, most sacred sanctum of a temple.</note>, in secret places regarded as particularly holy, where no one was permitted to enter except the priests or priestesses. 

This was not, however, merely a simple and common <term>elemental fire</term> <note>In alchemical thought, "elemental fire" is the physical flame used for cooking or industry, whereas "philosophical" or "celestial" fire is a more refined, spiritualized heat.</note> such as is found everywhere in the world, made from wood, coal, straw, and peat for cooking, baking, brewing, and other common work involving fire used daily. Nor was it ignited by fire struck with steel from coarse, horn-colored field-stones and flint.

<margin>
**SUN FIRE.**
<note>original: "IGNIS solaris."</note>
**Tinder.**
<note>original: "Fomes."</note>
</margin>

Instead, it was a fire that—through and by the sun’s hot and nearly fiery rays—was kindled purely and cleanly into tinder or other *felicem* <note>original: "felicem"; Latin for "lucky" or "favored." Here it refers to a "blessed" material that catches fire easily.</note> (as they called it back then), which is to say, easily-ignited material...

In this period, "chemist" and "alchemist" were often used interchangeably to describe those working in the laboratory.</note>—both those considered highly learned and very experienced, as well as among the common folk.

<margin>
**Guardianship:**
The preservation of this Fire of the Sages.
</margin>
In Rome, it was maintained for a long time with great fear and reverence, without being extinguished, by 20 chaste and pure virgins (because "bright virginity is a thing most pleasing to the gods" <note>original Latin: "Candida virginitas res eſt gratiſſima Divis"</note>, for the reason that pure virginity is acceptable to God and good spirits). This was kept in the most secret and holiest-regarded chapel or sacristy of the temple of their fabricated goddess **Vesta**—that is, the Heavenly Fire. 

<margin>
There were two Vestas: one was Earth, the mother of Saturn; the other, younger one, was the pure fire of the ether. <note>original Latin: "Duæ fuerũt VESTÆ, Altera Saturni mater TERRA Altera Iunior, Ignis Ætheris purus."</note>
</margin>
This name is derived from the Hebrew <term>Esch-Iah</term> <note>original Hebrew: אש יה (Esh Yah), literally "Fire of God". The author suggests that pagan names like Vesta were actually corrupted versions of sacred Hebrew terms.</note>, which means "Fire of God" <note>original Latin: "IGNIS DEI"</note>, or God’s Fire. The heathens fashioned their "Vesta" from this, as a mangled imitation—as if it were "Echia"—and named it accordingly.

# Philosophical Fire

The method of lighting and relighting the **Vestal Fire** from the Sun <note>original Latin/German: "IGNIS Vestalis à Sole accendendus & reaccendendus modus: Manier vnd weise der anzündung vnd wideranzündung deß Vestalischen Fewrs."</note>.

Afterward, however, the aforementioned **Vestal Fire** would be lit anew using a concave, triangular, and highly polished mirror (the likes of which—including round ones—I have seen among craftsmen who are good acquaintances of mine, and have even owned myself) or a vessel made of bronze or **bell-metal** <term>Bell-metal: A hard alloy of copper and tin typically used for casting bells, known for its ability to be polished to a high shine.</term> (positioned toward the sun). The instrument remained standing in this way until, through the gathering and the back-and-forth bouncing of the many hot sunbeams, the heat within it increased and, gathered in a **focus** <note>original: "Centro"</note>, was reflected back. Thus, tinder or some other parched, dry, and easily flammable material, placed directly opposite and facing the heated vessel, would catch and burst into flame.

Principles <term>the three fundamental alchemical components: Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt</term>, from every physical thing, can be separated freely upon a table. "Of the Celestial and Solar [fire]," he says, "the properties are a **Heat** that makes all things fruitful, and **Light**, which bestows life upon all. Of the **INFERNAL** (that is, the Elemental, such as from wood) **FIRE**, the properties are a **Burning** that consumes all, and a **darkness** that fills all things with sterility. Therefore, the Celestial and Luminous **FIRE** puts to flight the dark Demons; and even our **WOODEN** (that is, infernal) fire also puts them to flight, insofar as it possesses an image and vehicle of the **HIGHER LIGHT**." The same author in chapter 21, book 2 of the cited work: "Just as the **SUN** by its light drives away the darkness of night, so also does it drive away any powers of darkness whatsoever."

<margin>
*The flight of the spirits of darkness.* <note>original: "Fuga Spirituum tenebrarum"</note>
</margin>

<margin>
*Namely those who, out of their own malice, attempt to destroy or hinder our Philosophical work in the Oratory and Laboratory.* <term>Alchemists often combined "Oratory" (prayer) and "Laboratory" (physical work), believing that spiritual purity was as necessary as chemical expertise.</term> <note>original: "Illos nimirum, qui malitia ſua propria, conantur deſtruere vel impedire opus noſtrū Philoſophicum."</note>
</margin>

In German, then: the properties of the Upper (that is, the Celestial and Solar) Fire are a warmth that makes all things fruitful and a Light that gives life to all. The properties of the Lower (that is, the Elemental, meaning wood, coal, oil, <term>etc.</term>) Fire are an all-consuming heat and a darkness—that is, a coarseness and impurity—which simultaneously [fills] everything...

<summary>This page contrasts "Celestial Fire" (life-giving and spiritual) with "Elemental Fire" (consuming and physical), citing Agrippa to explain how even physical fire can repel dark spirits if it carries the "Higher Light." It emphasizes the dual nature of alchemical work occurring in both the prayer space and the workshop.</summary>

# Philosophical Fire

...immediately fills everything with sterility. The bright and clear Celestial Fire drives away evil spirits; our lower fire—that is, wood-fire—also does so, provided it acts as a vehicle for the upper light and thus bears a likeness to it. And in the 21st Chapter of the Second Book, in the cited work <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa, *De Occulta Philosophia*, first published in 1531.</note>, he says the following: "Just as the Sun with its light drives away the darkness of night, so also does it drive away all power of darkness and evil spirits." Thus far Agrippa.

<margin>
You, who are inexperienced in the secrets of Nature and a mocker of the truth, may stay at home with your fantasy—thinking the evil spirit departs on its own—for you only lead people into superstition.
</margin>

And indeed, experience has also proven this: when a light or candle, ignited by the Sun <note>This likely refers to using a "burning glass" or lens to focus solar rays and light a wick, a process considered more "pure" than striking flint.</note>, was kept burning near people possessed by evil spirits—allowing the light to be reflected upon them—the spirit, being a spirit of darkness and a hinderer and destroyer of all things good, began to rage, storm, and finally retreat. This occurred because of a **singular antipathy** <note>original Latin: *antipathiam singularem*</note>, or a particular opposition of presence, as the spirit could not endure the presence of that Solar Light.

# On External Heating.
<sig>42</sig>

# Philosophical Fire.

The diligent reader (especially following this, my brotherly urging) will easily know how to take note of the philosophical speeches and teachings for himself while reading through the books of the Sages. It would take much too long, and it is also unnecessary, to set everything down here in greater detail. From now on, let each person pay attention to these things themselves.

<margin>A silent objection is answered <note>original: "Tacite respondetur obiectioni"</note>. This should be in the same measure as the Sun warms when it resides in **Aries**, **Taurus**, **Gemini**, **Cancer**, or **Leo** <note>The author is referring to the "Degrees of Fire." Just as the sun grows hotter as it moves through these zodiac signs from spring to summer, the alchemist must gradually increase the heat of the furnace as the Work progresses</note>. This is certainly not to be neglected or set aside in our chemical labors or works; rather, it is highly necessary to observe it diligently. <meta>The sentence concludes on the next page with the word "brought."</meta> There is no need to include the words of other texts here, as those which speak of the essential heat of the **Sun** <note>original: "de calore SOLIS Essentiali"</note> and its warmth are already too clear.

# Concerning the External.

Since the previously cited texts—the philosophical sayings I have quoted—are so clear, distinct, and bright, and since all my other proofs in this present **Treatise** are truly philosophical in their teaching and entirely consistent with nature, we must proceed from these foundations. The **Olympic Solar Fire** <term>Vulcanus Solympicus: a poetic term for the alchemist's fire, named after Vulcan, the god of the forge, and "Olympic" to signify its solar or celestial quality</term> must be applied essentially and substantially to the **Highest Physical Work of the Alchemists** <note>original: "Opere Physico Chymicorum summo"</note>. This is to be done in such a way and manner—understood both literally and through comparison—that the aforementioned **degrees of heat** <note>original: "Caloris gradibus"</note> are applied artfully and as a matter of the highest necessity, just as I teach here in the preceding and following sections.

<margin>The supreme necessity of Solar Fire in the ripening of various fruits.</margin>

All that has been said is performed by the **Solar Fire**! In the Great World <note>The Macrocosm, or the natural universe at large</note>, this fire "cooks" all kinds of **Natural Fruits** from the outside. These fruits require external warmth to be ripened, matured, and promoted to their ultimate perfection and completion.

# Philosophical Fire

Es

Why should we then muddle about, or why should we henceforth linger so long in the **Spagyric Art** <term>Spagyric Art: a term for alchemy that emphasizes the separation and recombination of substances to purify them</term>, primarily in the preparation of the **Philosophical UNIVERSAL Stone**—and especially, in its proper time, in the **second OPERATION** <note>Also known as the "After-work," this is the stage following the initial preparation of the materials</note> or subsequent work? When **FIRE & AZOTH** <term>Azoth: considered by alchemists to be the universal solvent or the "mercury" of the philosophers; the essential life force of matter</term> are sufficient, along with **INFERNAL FIRE** <note>original: "Quando IGNIS & AZOTH sufficiunt, cum IGNE INFERNO"</note>—why should we work with the lower, elemental, coarse, destructive, sterilizing, and ruinous fire **NAKEDLY & SIMPLY** <note>original: "NUDE & SIMPLICITER"</note>, purely, poorly, and alone?

Regarding that fire, I say—alongside **H. C. Agrippa** <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535), an influential German polymath, physician, and occult writer</note>—that it does not preserve life. Instead, to all naturally composed or assembled things to which it is applied in **Chemistry**, it causes an un-philosophical, cruel, and ruinous destruction in a visibly divisive manner. It brings about a final and complete downfall that reduces things to nothing, and promotes the ultimate, everlasting death.

->### Of Philosophical Fire ###<-

...of the infernal or elemental [fire] (in and of itself) the properties are: an All-Consuming ardor, and a darkness filling all things with sterility. To put it in German: For this reason—as also H. C. Agrippa <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535), a famous Renaissance polymath and occult philosopher cited here as an authority on the nature of elements</note> has been cited above—the properties of our lower or coarse elemental fire (when considered by and for itself alone) are an all-consuming heat and a darkness that fills everything at once with infertility. In contrast, the properties of the Upper, or rather Heavenly and Solar Fire, are a warmth that makes all things fruitful and a Light that gives life to everyone.

# Philosophical Fire #

F iij

<margin>The inevitable necessity of Solar-Olympic <term>Vulcan</term> <note>In alchemy, "Vulcan" refers to the element of fire or the personification of the transformative heat used in the laboratory</note> in the preparation of the Philosophers' Stone:</margin>

The **Solar-Olympic** fire is very necessary to be observed and applied in <term>Spagyry</term> <note>The alchemical art of separating and recombining the constituents of bodies, often to create medicines</note> by every artist who does not wish to "thresh empty straw" <note>An idiom meaning to engage in useless, unproductive labor</note> and spend his good time with loss, disgrace, scorn, and mockery in vain within the art—that is, the attainment of the **Universal** from the **proper Matter** <note>original Latin: *Vniversale ex debita Materia*; refers to the creation of the Philosophers' Stone from the correct starting substance</note>, from true Matter, according to God's will and blessing, and artistically in accordance with Nature. If one does not do this, they will only become "children of experience" <note>A phrase implying those who learn only through painful trial and error rather than by following established wisdom</note>: finally, then, when you have become somewhat wiser through your losses, you will at last—though **perhaps far too late** <note>original Latin: *at fortè nimis serò*</note>—consider how faithfully I, Dr. Henricus Khunrath, have meant it for you through this my philosophic and right-teaching discourse in this treatise.

<margin>The unavoidable and indispensable necessity of the High Fire or Olympic—that is, Heavenly—Vulcan, in the preparation of the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>The "Great Work" of alchemy: a substance believed to transmute base metals and heal all diseases</note> from <term>Azoth</term> <note>The essential mercurial spirit or universal solvent</note>.</margin>

# Of Fire #

...ought to be sufficient, for one should be satisfied with <term>Azoth</term> <note>The universal solvent or "Mercury of the Philosophers," believed to be the essential agent of transformation</note> and Fire) the **Solar** cooperations—the joint-workings of the Sun (just as they occur in the Great World or Macrocosm)—must be applied; otherwise there will be no good end; that is the "end of the song" <note>A German idiom meaning the final result or the conclusion of the matter</note>.

<margin>How the sayings of the Philosophers regarding Fire are to be properly understood:</margin>
All the sayings and teachings of the Philosophers concerning **Fire** <note>original Latin: *de Igne*</note> are not only to be understood as referring to the proper degree of **Fire's** heat and its just governance—that is, its external Quantity or Quality; the convenient degree and correct regulation of the fire's warmth, which is its external strength or weakness, manner, or method. Rather, they are to be understood simultaneously with the nature and internal quality of the Fire <note>The Latin text here reads *qualitate externa* (external quality), but the German translation provided in the text correctly identifies this as *innerlicher Eigenschafft* (internal quality), which fits the philosophical contrast being made</note>; that which has been sufficiently proven by me in the present Treatise—concerning the Fire's nature and inner property, power, virtue, and effect: as has been sufficiently demonstrated and shown by me in this present little book.

When both the **Infernal Fire** <note>original: *Ignis Infernus*; refers here to the "lower" elemental fire of the furnace or laboratory stove</note> (that is, the Elemental) and the **Celestial Sun** are joined **Physico-Magically** <note>A term used by Khunrath to describe his unique blend of natural science, philosophy, and divine magic</note> into one co-

### Philosophical Fire ###

...the year 1580, first printed in an open letter <note>original: "Sendschreiben"; a public epistolary work</note> by one who calls himself **Johannes Pontanus** <note>an alchemist famous for his 'Epistle on the Philosophical Fire,' in which he claimed that after many failures, he discovered the secret fire through deep study</note>, concerning the **philosophical fire** <note>the secret heat or catalyst used in alchemy, which many authors claim is not a literal flame</note> and the preparation of the **Philosopher’s Stone** thereby, it is read in the following words: 

Our **FIRE** <term>IGNIS</term> <note>original: "IGNIS"; Latin for fire</note>, he says, is mineral; it is uniform and continuous; it does not evaporate unless it is excessively agitated; it partakes of the nature of **Sulphur**; it is taken from a source other than the matter itself; it destroys all things; it dissolves and congeals, and likewise it calcines <note>to reduce a substance to powder through heat</note>; it is discovered through artifice and is composed at no expense, or at least very little. And that fire works with a moderate heat; it performs all the necessary sublimations at once; and through deep and profound meditation alone is that fire found. 

In German, it reads thus: Our Fire, he says, is mineral, uniform, steady, and does not dissipate, provided it is not overheated or made too strong. It is a partaker and companion of **Sulphur**: It is taken from somewhere else than from the **MATTER** <term>MATERIA</term> <note>the raw, base substance intended for transformation into the Philosopher's Stone</note>;

# Concerning the External #

...it crushes everything; it dissolves and **congeals** <note>to solidify or thicken a liquid</note>; likewise, it also **calcines** <note>to reduce a substance to powder through heat</note>. Such a fire, he says, is the **principal agent** <term>PROPRIUM AGENS</term> <note>original Latin: "proprium agens"; the primary active force or "worker" in a chemical reaction</note>, the actual worker in the **philosophical work** of their secret **Stone**.

### Philosophical Fire ###

...nevertheless (though speaking loftily, yet very recklessly swearing by the living God in the process) shall be converted into a completely pure substance or **Matter** <term>MATERIA</term> through his aforementioned fire. And whoever separates anything of that kind from the true **subject** <term>SUBIECTO</term> <note>original: "subiecto"; the raw material or starting substance chosen for the alchemical process</note>, believing it to be necessary, truly knows nothing of **Philosophy** <note>original: "Philosophia"; refers here to Alchemical Philosophy or the "Art"</note>. For, he says, <margin>Note these words diligently.</margin> that which is superfluous, unclean, shameful, and drossy—and finally the whole substance of the subject—is perfected into a **spiritual, fixed body** <term>CORPUS SPIRITUALE</term> <note>a "fixed" body is one that can withstand the heat of the fire without evaporating; a "spiritual" body suggests a perfected, subtle state of matter</note> through the mediation of our **FIRE**; and the Sages were never ignorant of this. For this reason, few reach the Art, because they believe there is something superfluous or unclean [that must be purged]: since that Fire separates nothing from the matter; it does not divide the pure parts from the impure (as all [other] Philosophers say), but converts the entire subject into purity. Therefore, the error in this Art is failing to find the Fire that converts the whole matter into the true **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>LAPIDEM PHILOSOPHORUM</term>. # Analysis of the 4 Figures

In the first figure, he encompasses all three lights: **Natural**, **Supernatural**, and **Divine**. Or: **Natural**, **Angelic**, and **Divine**. That is: **Magic** <term>Magia: here referring to the study of the hidden secrets of nature</term>, **Cabala** <term>Cabala: the study of divine mysteries through sacred names and scripture</term>, and **Theology** <term>Theologia: the study of God and divine revelation</term>; and he encloses all three within the first single figure for the sake of the **Holy Trinity**. 

And there are the **Hebrew Names** which stand in the circle: first, **God's names**, or divine power. For the names of God are divine powers, of which there are 72 names of God <note>This refers to the *Shem HaMephorash*, a hidden name of God in Cabalistic tradition derived from Exodus</note>. But how they are to be used, no one knows except a **Cabalist**; and **Cabala** deals primarily with the divine names. From these names of God now flow the names of the **angels**, which also stand in the first figure within the circle, and they are nothing other than **angelic powers**. Upon these angelic powers now hang the natural powers of the **firmament**, the **powers of the heavens** <note>original Latin: "Virtutes Coelorum"</note>, of which the Lord says: "The powers of the heavens shall be shaken." <note>A biblical reference to Matthew 24:29 or Luke 21:26 regarding the end times</note>

It describes a celestial hierarchy where the 72 names of God empower the angels, who in turn govern the physical forces of the firmament.</summary>

# Judgment on the 4 Figures

<meta>catchword: Spirit</meta>

These are the **astral names** <term>astral names: names relating to the stars and their spiritual influences on earthly matter</term> of this work, or its astronomical names.

In philosophical terms, however, in the first operation of the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Lapis Philosophorum: the legendary "masterwork" of alchemy, believed to transform base metals into gold and grant spiritual perfection</term>, only a pure water is produced. For water is the beginning of all natural things. From this water, in the second operation, the dry part—the earth—is separated, just as GOD in the Beginning separated the earth from the water. In the work of the Stone, this is the **Salt** <note>original: "Sal"</note>, snow-white, and it is the **Moon** <note>original: "Luna"</note>. From this snow-white Salt, in the third operation, the **Philosopher’s Sulfur** <note>original: "Sulphur Philosophorum"</note> is produced, which is blood-red. Thus you have, from the three operations: **Liquid** <note>original: "Liquorem"</note>, **Salt**, and **Sulfur**; or in the manner of the elements: **Water** <note>original: "Aquam"</note>, **Air** <note>original: "Aërem"</note>, and **Fire** <note>original: "Ignem"</note>.

In all three, however, the air exists as a **Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"</note>, just as in the natural earth there is water, fire, and air—this air is their spirit and life. In **microcosmic** <term>Microcosmic: referring to the human being as a "miniature universe" that mirrors the laws of the great cosmos</term> terms, however, these three are called **Spirit**, **Body**, and **Soul**: The Spirit...

# Of the Great Amphitheater

...**Spirit** <note>original: "Geist"</note> first, from which a **Body** <note>original: "Leib"</note> was formed: and then thirdly, the **Soul** <note>original: "Seel"</note> is the **Perfection** <note>original: "Perfectio"</note> and clarified essence.

This is wonderfully prefigured in the third figure: but not one in a thousand will truly perceive <note>literally "taste"</note> it therein.

Regarding the **Fire**: it is a great mystery how the **Matter of the Stone** <term>Materia deß Lapidis: the base substance or "first matter" used to create the Philosopher's Stone</term> is transmuted from one essence into another. And it is no natural fire. All the claims of the Alchemists are lies when they fool people with their fanciful **Degrees of Fire** <note>original: "Gradibus Ignis"</note>. Rather (as I tell my Lord confidentially), it is an **Astral Fire**, an **Ethereal Fire**, and it comes through **Magic** <note>original: "Magiam"</note>, and can only be brought about through the same—otherwise, it is impossible for all eternity. Therefore, all who have possessed the **Stone** <note>original: "Lapidem"</note> have been **Magicians** <term>Magi: in this context, spiritual philosophers who understand the hidden laws of nature and the divine</term>. From this comes the fable of the heathens, that **Prometheus** climbed toward heaven and stole his fire from **Jove** <note>original: "Iovi"; referring to the Roman god Jupiter</note>. From this also, the **Persians** held fire to be their God.

# Judgment on the Four Figures

...[to hold] it, because they saw that their **Magicians** <note>original: "Magi"</note> and **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> accomplished so many great things with it.

And those fools, the **Persians**, did not understand these **secrets** <note>original: "Secreta"</note> of their philosophers; they therefore made **fire** <note>original: "Fewer"</note> into an idol, just as happened in **Ur** in **Chaldea**. For **Ur** <term>Ur: a Hebrew/Chaldean word for "fire" or "light"</term> signifies "Fire" in the Chaldean language, and this city in Chaldea received its name for that reason: because the philosophers who possessed this marvelous fire lived there.

And the light in the **breastplate of Aaron** <note>original: "Brustschiltlein Aaronis"; referring to the priestly Hoshen mentioned in the Book of Exodus</note> is also called **Ur**, or **Urim** <term>Urim: part of the "Urim and Thummim," sacred objects used by the ancient Israelite high priests to seek divine oracular answers</term>. From this it has come to pass that the ancient **Persian kings**, whenever they traveled, had the fire carried before them on a white horse. But these kings were not such fools as to have common fire carried before them; rather, it was the **magical**, wondrous, most **secret**, hidden fire—a **secret of all Nature**.

<summary>The author explains that the "Ur" of the Chaldeans and the "Urim" of Aaron's breastplate are identical to the secret, magical fire of the alchemists, which the Persians mistakenly worshipped as a literal deity.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the author's analysis of the illustrative plates (figures) within the "Amphitheater of Eternal Wisdom," moving from the interpretation of the third figure to the fourth, which focuses on the spiritual practice of prayer.</meta>

# of the great Amphitheater.

And that is the interpretation of the third figure.

In the 4th figure, the Amphitheater teaches Theology in the Oratory <note>original: "in Oratorio"; a private place for prayer</note>, and Theology is nothing other than Divine Discourse <note>original: "Alloquium Divinum"</note>, a conversation with God through prayer and through the Holy Spirit.

And thus the Lord has, in the briefest form, my judgment <note>original: "Iudicium"</note> concerning the great Amphitheater: from which report of mine the Lord may see whether I understand anything in these matters or not. And I wish to share with the Lord at another time—if I live—a report concerning the magical transmutations <note>original: "Magischen Transmutationibus"</note>, such as how the Magicians <note>original: "Magii"</note> in Egypt turned their staffs into serpents and changed water into blood, <term>etc.</term> <note>referring to the biblical contest between Moses and the Pharaoh’s sorcerers in the Book of Exodus</note>. For indeed, unspeakable transmutations are contained within Magic <note>original: "Magia"</note>, and these transmutations occur in a magical way, and not otherwise.

Herewith, I commend the Lord to the dear God.

## Treatise II.

# THEOPHRASTUS
# PARACELSUS wrote this
# Little Treatise on the Tincture or Oil
# of Antimony <note>original Latin: "de Tinctura sive Oleo Antimonij"</note> for his dear, good
# Friend THEODORE N.
# In the year 1536, the 17th
# of May.

The ancient Spagyrists <term>Spagyri: alchemists who practiced the art of separating and recombining elements to create medicines</term> said quite rightly and justly, my dear Theodore: *“Whatever the wise seek is found in Mercury.”* <note>original Latin: "Est in Mercurio, Quicquid quærunt Sapientes"</note> But they did not clearly express what kind of Mercury <note>In alchemy, "Mercury" often refers to a philosophical principle or a specific substance rather than just the common metal quicksilver</note> they meant. Although I am not permitted to say whether I knew what they knew and intended, I am nevertheless satisfied by the visible proofs which, as it were, convince me that they meant exactly such a Mercury as the one I am writing about. For, since I, through this tincture, have helped many suffering from gout <term>Podagrischen: those afflicted with podagra, or gout of the foot</term>—

—insane, lepers, those struck by apoplexy, those with dropsy <term>Wassersucht: an old medical term for edema or swelling caused by excess fluid</term>, the falling sickness <term>epilepsy</term>, the French disease <note>original: "Franzosen"—a common historical term for syphilis</note>, plague, fever, and similar diseases <note>original Latin: "Morbis"</note>. Do with it as I have done, and you will surely attain the crown of health for the poor, from both God and man, in truth and with successful progress.

# ON THE TINCTURE
## *Of Antimony.*

Stibium <note>original: "Stibium," another name for the mineral antimony</note>, as the Philosophers say, is composed of the noble mineral Sulfur, and they have praised it as the secret black Lead of the Sages <note>original: "schwartze geheimbde Bley der Weysen"; in alchemy, "Lead of the Sages" refers to a primary material that, though seemingly base, contains the seed of gold or medicine</note>.

The Arabs in their language have called it *Asinat* or *Azinat*; the Alchemists retain the name *Antimonium*.

### *Addition.*

It is called *Antimonium* by the Moors; others call it *Alabastrum* or *tarbason*. To the Arabs and Spaniards it is *Alcohol* <note>The word "alcohol" originally referred to a very fine powder, specifically the powdered antimony used as eye makeup (kohl), long before it was applied to distilled spirits</note>. Avicenna <note>Ibn Sina (c. 980–1037), a Persian polymath whose medical texts were standard in European universities for centuries</note> calls it *Atemed* in Chapter 7. Pliny <note>Pliny the Elder (23–79 AD), the Roman author of "Natural History"</note> writes of Antimony in Book 33, Chapter 6. Dioscorides <note>Pedanius Dioscorides (c. 40–90 AD), a Greek physician and author of the most influential herbal of the pre-modern era</note> relates the preparation of Stibium in Book 5, Chapter 98. They also call it *Stibi*, *Stimmi*, etc. The Germans call it *Spießglaß*, or more correctly *Spießglantz* <note>meaning "spear-glance," a name derived from the long, needle-like crystals found in the mineral stibnite</note>, as Georg Fabricius <note>Georg Fabricius (1516–1571), a German scholar and poet known for his work on ancient history and minerals</note> opines. Gerlandius calls it *Aleo-*

sublimation <term>sublimation: the process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used for purification</term>; some believe they can easily succeed through reverberation <term>reverberation: the use of a furnace where the flame is "echoed" or reflected back onto the material to provide intense heat</term>, and thus obtain its great secret and perfect medicine <note>original: "perfectam Medicinam"</note>. For I tell you truly, without veiled speech: if you lose any of the aforementioned sulfur <note>In alchemy, "sulfur" refers to the flammable, oily principle of a substance, considered its "soul."</note> through the preparation and burning—as even a small fire may easily damage it—you have lost the true penetrating spirit. And take note of this as well.

## OF THE TINCTURE OF ANTIMONY ##

How could the body possibly turn into an oil, or yield its lovely oil, if it were reduced to the final state of its degree? For glass is, in all things, the ultimate and final essence.

As you should then know, all creatures dissolve into their final forms, and at the Judgment of the Last Day, they shall become amethysts and other lovely diamonds. According to the lineages of the twelve patriarchs, they shall be placed among the lineages of the twelve noble stones, which <term>Hermes</term> <note>Hermes Trismegistus: the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy and hermetic philosophy.</note> the Great mentions in his book. We have reported and taught extensively on such matters in our book of the <term>Cabala</term> <note>Cabala (Kabbalah): here referring to Paracelsus’s own mystical-philosophical interpretations of nature and scripture rather than traditional Jewish Kabbalah.</note>.

You should also know that you will by no means receive the perfect, noble oil when it is extracted by pouring <term>distilled vinegar</term> <note>original: "aceto correcto"</note> over the <term>antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimonium"</note>, nor even through <term>reverberation</term> <term>reverberation: a process of heating a substance in a furnace where the heat is "reverberated" or reflected back onto the material by the furnace roof.</term>. And even if many of its various colors should show themselves, it is still not

<meta>catchword: not</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that turning antimony into glass prevents the extraction of its medicinal oil and relates the transformation of matter to the biblical end times. ->PARACELSI.<-

...not the right way. You might well obtain an oil, but it possesses no part of the <term>tincture</term> <note>tincture: in alchemy, the concentrated spiritual essence or medicinal power of a substance.</note> or strength, or any transformative quality within it; this you should know.

Now we proceed to the practical manual work:

Take, in the name of God and the Eternal Trinity, a fine and very pure <term>ore of antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimonij mineram"</note>, which is beautifully white and solid, and filled on the inside with yellow flinty streaks or veins, and is also full of red and blue colors and little veins; this variety shall be the best. For our water is like the <term>ostrich</term> <note>ostrich: in Renaissance natural history, the ostrich was famously believed to be able to eat and digest iron due to the intense "heat" of its stomach.</note> in its nature, which digests all iron through its heat and consumes it to nothing. # ON THE TINCTURE OF ANTIMONY

...consume it and turn it into <term>slime</term> <note>original: "schlich"; a mining term referring to fine, silty ore residue or sludge after washing.</note>, so that it remains only as a yellow earth, and then it would be completely ruined.

Take this as an analogy from <term>Luna</term> <note>Luna: the alchemical name for silver.</note>, which, being beautiful, pure, and solid, is dissolved in these waters of ours. And even if you wished to reduce it back into a <term>solid body</term> <note>original: "gediegen corpus"</note>, you would not be able to do so. For it would remain before you as a pale, yellow earth, and sometimes it may well congeal together into a shape like horn—or a white horse’s hoof—which you cannot return to any solid body by any art <note>The author is likely describing the formation of silver chloride (known historically as "Luna Cornea" or Horn Silver), which has a waxy, horn-like appearance and was notoriously difficult for early chemists to convert back into metallic silver.</note>.

-># OF PARACELSUS<-

But every **silver** <note>original: "Luna"; the alchemical name for silver, associated with the moon.</note> contains within a **mark** <note>original: "Marck"; a traditional unit of weight for precious metals, roughly 8 ounces.</note> two **lots** <note>original: "Lot"; a small unit of weight, approximately 1/16th of a mark or half an ounce.</note> of **gold**, and some even more. For gold is so united with the **Lunar nature** <note>referring to the essential qualities of silver</note> that it cannot be separated out, either by **nitric acid** <term>aquafort</term> <note>original: "aquafort"; from *aqua fortis* or "strong water," an acid used to part silver from gold.</note> or common **antimony**, as the goldsmiths know.

But when the aforementioned mixture is thrown upon the silver while it is in **flux** <note>original: "fluß"; the molten or liquid state of the metal.</note>, then such a **separation** <note>original: "scheidung".</note> occurs that the silver readily gives up its internal gold in **nitric acid** or **aqua regia** <term>regal</term> <note>original: "regal"; from *aqua regia* or "royal water," a powerful mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold.</note>. It allows itself to be separated from the gold, casting the gold to the bottom and letting it fall; something which otherwise can and may by no means happen. Therefore, this process is not an **addition** <note>original: "einbringen"; implying the gold was added to the silver by the alchemist.</note>, but an **extraction** <note>original: "außbringen"; the drawing out of what was already there.</note> from the silver.

<summary>Paracelsus explains that silver naturally contains trace amounts of gold that are normally inseparable, but can be extracted using a specific antimony-based flux. After ten days, give it the heat of the second degree <note>Alchemists typically used four "degrees" of heat, ranging from the warmth of a brooding hen to the intense heat of a fusion furnace.</note>; thus the gray and black color will transform bit by bit into a white one, so that it becomes **heavenly white**.
